and that framework materially changes diagnosis, differential ranking, risk stratification, or management:

You MUST generate at least ONE dedicated High-Yield card encoding that framework.

(If Sectioned Build mode is active, this requirement is QUEUED and must be emitted only in SECTION G.)

Rules:
• Use this only for source-supported frameworks that improve interpretation.
• Prefer frameworks with approximately 3-5 major branches.
• Do NOT convert sprawling ontologies or long textbook taxonomies into giant recall lists.
• Do NOT create a framework card if the source does not clearly teach a usable framework.
• Preserve the source's actual branch labels whenever possible instead of renaming them into vague buckets.
• Each branch in the answer must include a short discriminator, risk pivot, or management pivot.
• Keep the answer compact enough for active recall: usually 3-5 bullets total.
• Prefer one anchor framework card over multiple fragmented subtype cards unless the source also gives distinct threshold or management pivots worth separating.
• The goal is structural recall: after reviewing the card, the learner should be able to recreate the article's main subtype/risk map, not just recite disconnected facts.

COMPARISON CARD RULE

Generate a compare/contrast High-Yield card ONLY when the comparison changes diagnosis, differential ranking,
risk stratification, management, or exam selection.

Good examples:
• adenoma versus FNH when hepatobiliary-phase interpretation changes management confidence
• inflammatory versus HNF1A subtype when the distinction changes expected behavior or risk
• benign-appearing lesion versus malignant-risk subtype when surveillance/resection thresholds change

Do NOT generate comparison cards that are merely interesting descriptive contrasts without a decision pivot.

Recommended format:

High_Yield_Q:
“For [ENTITY], what diagnostic/risk framework changes recognition or management?”

High_Yield_A:
• [Bucket / subtype / branch 1] → [1-line discriminator or risk pivot]
• [Bucket / subtype / branch 2] → [1-line discriminator or risk pivot]
• [Bucket / subtype / branch 3] → [1-line discriminator or risk pivot]
• (extend only if the source clearly supports a compact 4th or 5th branch)
• Exam pivot: [one line: how this framework organizes recognition or management]

If the framework is larger than 5 branches:
• compress it to the highest-yield source-supported branches only, if that remains faithful
• otherwise do NOT generate the framework card

-----------------------------------------------------

REPORT-CRITICAL / MANAGEMENT-PIVOT CARD RULE

Generate a report-critical High-Yield card only when a source-supported finding changes what the radiologist must inspect, report, recommend, communicate, or escalate.

This card type is for actionable interpretation, not generic reporting advice.

Generate a separate independent High-Yield TSV row when the finding changes:
• urgency or acuity
• immediate communication
• treatment versus conservative management
• surgery, drainage, embolization, anticoagulation, or other intervention
• operative planning
• staging or resectability
• prognosis or risk stratification
• follow-up interval or surveillance pathway
• complication status
• required report elements for clinically useful interpretation

Do NOT generate a report-critical card for generic statements such as:
• "report the location"
• "describe the lesion"
• "mention complications"
• "correlate clinically"

unless the source names a specific finding whose presence changes management, outcome, staging, urgency, or follow-up.

Recommended question forms:
• "In [ENTITY], which imaging finding should be reported because it changes [management/staging/urgency/follow-up]?"
• "For [ENTITY], what must be inspected on imaging because [specific consequence]?"
• "In suspected [ENTITY], which findings support urgent [intervention/escalation]?"

Avoid:
• "What should you report for this disease?"
• "What is important about this topic?"
• "What management facts were included in the source?"

High_Yield_A should include:
• Report-critical finding: [source-supported feature]
• Consequence: [what changes clinically]
• Imaging specificity / limitation: [if the finding is nonspecific or needs correlation]

-----------------------------------------------------

Inflation Rule:

Do NOT generate high-yield cards for:

• Redundant facts
• Minor epidemiology
• Non-decision-impacting details
• Restatements of image findings
• Content already encoded in another high-yield card

Each high-yield card must represent a distinct board pivot.

-----------------------------------------------------

Integrity Rule:

High-Yield cards must NOT:

• Duplicate content from Mechanism cards
• Duplicate content from Differential Drill logic
• Leak diagnosis-specific pattern recognition
• Expand beyond what is explicitly supported in Core / RadPrimer / STATdx / Radiopaedia

Quality over quantity.

-----------------------------------------------------
DIAGNOSTIC PERFORMANCE & THRESHOLD RULE
(MUTUAL RECALL + SOURCE TRANSPARENCY + ACTIVE REVIEW REQUIREMENT)

This applies to High-Yield cards that encode:

• Sensitivity
• Specificity
• PPV / NPV
• Accuracy
• Likelihood ratios
• HU cutoffs
• SUV thresholds
• Size thresholds
• Enhancement thresholds
• Quantitative staging criteria
• Any numeric diagnostic performance metric
• Comparative lab-test performance when board-relevant
• Best-test performance in a specific clinical setting when board-relevant

-----------------------------------------------------

0) ACTIVE REVIEW REQUIREMENT (MANDATORY)

During SECTION G, the assistant MUST explicitly review whether the topic has
diagnostic performance data worth encoding.

This review must include, when relevant:

• Imaging test sensitivity / specificity
• Comparative performance of one modality versus another
• Lab-test performance when it materially affects diagnosis
• “Best test” or “most useful test” performance in a specific clinical setting
• Quantitative thresholds that change interpretation

If such data are:
• explicitly present in Core / RadPrimer / STATdx / Radiopaedia
  → encode them in SECTION G
• absent from those sources but likely high-yield and clinically important
  → the assistant SHOULD consider external literature under the external-literature exception
• absent, weak, highly heterogeneous, conflicting, or not exam-relevant
  → do NOT force generation

The goal is to capture stable, high-impact diagnostic performance pivots,
not to accumulate low-value statistics.

-----------------------------------------------------

1) MUTUAL RECALL STRUCTURE (ALLOWED & PREFERRED)

If multiple performance metrics are tightly linked
(e.g., sensitivity + specificity),
they MAY be encoded on the SAME card
to enforce mutual recall.

Example format:

High_Yield_Q:
“What are the sensitivity and specificity of CT for X?”

High_Yield_A:
• Sensitivity: ___
• Specificity: ___
• Exam pivot: ___

This is preferred when:
• The metrics are commonly tested together
• The pairing improves recall strength
• Both metrics are equally exam-relevant

-----------------------------------------------------

2) SOURCE PRIORITY

Primary source hierarchy still applies:

1) Core Radiology
2) RadPrimer
3) STATdx
4) Radiopaedia

If performance metrics are NOT provided in those sources:

You MAY include externally sourced values
ONLY if:

• They come from a credible medical source
  (major review, textbook, guideline, meta-analysis).
• They are widely accepted values (not single small studies).
• They are stable enough to improve test-taking or interpretation.
• They are clearly labeled as outside-source.

In that case, you MUST include the following lines inside High_Yield_A:

“External literature source (not stated in Core/RadPrimer/STATdx/Radiopaedia).”
“Source: [stable link]”

Both lines must appear explicitly in High_Yield_A.
Silent external sourcing is prohibited.

The source link must point to the exact article, guideline, review, or reference supporting the card.
Acceptable links include:
• DOI URL
• PubMed URL
• journal article landing page
• guideline page

If multiple sources were reviewed, use the single most directly supporting source for the final wording of the card.

If no stable source link can be provided:
• Do NOT generate the externally sourced High-Yield card.

-----------------------------------------------------

3) EXACTNESS RULE

• Preserve numbers exactly as written in the source.
• Preserve ranges exactly.
• Preserve “approximately” wording if used.
• Do NOT round.
• Do NOT merge multiple studies.
• Do NOT average values.

If multiple external sources conflict:
• Do NOT generate the card.

-----------------------------------------------------

4) EXAM PIVOT REQUIREMENT

Every performance card MUST include:

??? One short line:
  ???Exam pivot: ____.???

This must explain how the number changes test-taking or interpretation.

If no pivot exists:
??? Do NOT generate the card.

4B) NEGATIVE-EVIDENCE META CARD BAN

Do NOT generate a High-Yield card whose main point is merely that:
??? no sensitivity/specificity was provided
??? no PPV/NPV was stated
??? the source did not report likelihood ratios
??? the source review did not find performance metrics

Absence of metrics is NOT itself a High-Yield retrieval target.

Instead:
??? if source-supported quantitative exceptions, thresholds, or imaging percentages exist, ask those directly
??? if no actionable number exists, generate no performance card
??? do NOT replace a missing performance card with a meta-review card

5) INFLATION CONTROL

Do NOT generate performance cards when:

• Numbers are vague or weakly stated.
• The metric does not influence interpretation.
• The number is trivial or rarely tested.
• The performance varies so widely that no stable pivot exists.
• The metric is too technique-dependent to support a durable board-style card.

Performance cards exist to encode high-impact diagnostic metrics,
not to accumulate statistics.

-----------------------------------------------------

6) SECTION G PERFORMANCE SUBBLOCK REQUIREMENT

Within SECTION G, diagnostic performance cards should be treated as an explicit subblock
whenever the source review or permitted external review yields stable, board-relevant values.

These cards remain part of SECTION G.
Do NOT create a separate section for performance unless the user explicitly requests one.

SECTION: ULTRASOUND APPEARANCE RULES
FILE:    8B_ultrasoundappearance.txt

ULTRASOUND APPEARANCE ENCODING RULE (MANDATORY WHEN SOURCE SUPPORTS)

If the provided Core/RadPrimer/STATdx source explicitly describes ultrasound appearance
(echogenicity, echotexture, posterior acoustic behavior, Doppler vascularity, compressibility, cystic-versus-solid morphology, dynamic maneuver behavior, contrast-enhanced ultrasound behavior, source-stated sonographic signs/artifacts, or ultrasound-specific discriminator features):

You MUST generate at least ONE dedicated High-Yield card encoding that ultrasound appearance.

(If Sectioned Build mode is active, this requirement is QUEUED and must be emitted only in SECTION G.)

Rules:
- Ultrasound details may be included ONLY if explicitly stated in the source.
- Include the sonographic feature exactly as written when possible.
- Include Doppler, posterior acoustic behavior, compressibility, mobility, wall layering, maneuver findings, or source-named artifacts/signs ONLY if explicitly stated.
- Include contrast-enhanced ultrasound behavior ONLY if explicitly stated, including enhancement phase and washout behavior when provided.
- Do NOT infer Doppler behavior unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer posterior acoustic enhancement or shadowing unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer compressibility unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer wall stratification unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer CEUS enhancement, washout, or vascularity unless source-supported.
- Do NOT infer mobility, sludge/debris behavior, twinkle, ring-down, comet-tail, reverberation, or other ultrasound-specific artifact/sign behavior unless source-supported.
- Do NOT generalize beyond the specific source statement.
- Keep the card tight: 2-6 short lines max.

Recommended format:

High_Yield_Q:
"For [ENTITY], what ultrasound, Doppler, or CEUS pattern should be recognized?"

High_Yield_A:
- Sonographic feature: [source-stated appearance]
- Doppler / acoustic / compressibility / dynamic feature: [only if explicitly stated]
- CEUS behavior: [enhancement phase or washout behavior only if explicitly stated]
- Imaging specificity: [Diagnostic / Strongly suggestive / Nonspecific / Requires correlation, only when relevant]
- Recognition pivot: [one line: why this helps recognize or discriminate]

If the ultrasound description is too long:
- Split into multiple High-Yield cards ONLY if each card is its own distinct exam pivot.
- Otherwise omit lower-value details.

NUCLEAR MEDICINE APPEARANCE ENCODING RULE (MANDATORY WHEN SOURCE SUPPORTS)

If the provided Core/RadPrimer/STATdx source explicitly describes nuclear medicine appearance
(for example tracer uptake or non-uptake, photopenia, warm-versus-cold behavior, scintigraphic distribution, PET avidity, retention, washout, excretion, phase/timing, biodistribution, tracer-specific discriminator behavior, or source-named nuclear medicine pivots):

You MUST generate at least ONE dedicated High-Yield card encoding that nuclear medicine appearance.

(If Sectioned Build mode is active, this requirement is QUEUED and must be emitted only in SECTION G.)

Rules:
- Nuclear medicine details may be included ONLY if explicitly stated in the source.
- Include the tracer or exam name exactly as written when possible.
- Include uptake, photopenia, avidity, retention, washout, excretion, phase/timing, or physiologic biodistribution only if source-supported.
- Do NOT infer uptake, photopenia, avidity, washout, retention, excretion, timing, or physiologic distribution unless source-supported.
- Do NOT generalize one tracer's behavior to another tracer.
- Do NOT convert source silence into a negative finding.
- Keep the card tight: 2-6 short lines max.

Recommended format:

High_Yield_Q:
"For [ENTITY], what tracer-specific nuclear medicine or PET/SPECT pattern should be recognized?"

High_Yield_A:
- Tracer / exam: [source-stated tracer or study]
- Appearance: [source-stated uptake, photopenia, avidity, retention, washout, excretion, distribution, or other behavior]
- Timing / phase: [only if explicitly stated]
- Imaging specificity: [Diagnostic / Strongly suggestive / Nonspecific / Requires correlation, only when relevant]
- Recognition pivot: [one line: why this helps recognize or discriminate]

If the nuclear medicine description is too long:
- Split into multiple High-Yield cards ONLY if each card is its own distinct exam pivot.
- Otherwise omit lower-value details.

SECTIONED BUILD RULE (QUALITY CONTROL)

SECTIONED BUILD OVERRIDE (QUEUE RULE â€” CRITICAL)

When Mode 1 Sectioned Review Output is active:

â€¢ Any rule elsewhere that says â€œMUST generateâ€ a card type
  (e.g., syndrome high-yield pairs, mechanism requirements, traps, etc.)
  MUST be treated as a QUEUED REQUIREMENT.

Meaning:
â€¢ The assistant MUST NOT emit cards outside the currently active section.
â€¢ Instead, it must queue the requirement and generate it only when the workflow reaches that section.

Examples:
â€¢ If a syndrome is introduced during SECTION A (PRIMARY UNKNOWN),
  the required syndrome High-Yield pair MUST be generated later in:
  SECTION G (High-Yield), not immediately.

â€¢ If a mechanism is implied/required during SECTION A,
  it must be generated later in:
  SECTION D (Mechanism), not immediately.

â€¢ If a Boards Trap is implied during any section,
  it MUST be generated later in:
  SECTION E (Boards Trap), not immediately.

â€¢ If epidemiology pivots exist in the source,
  they MUST be generated later in:
  SECTION F (Epidemiology), not immediately.

This rule supersedes all â€œgenerate immediatelyâ€ language elsewhere.
Cards must be generated and reviewed in discrete sections.
Do NOT generate â€œeverything in one shot.â€

Section order is STRICT:

SECTION A) PRIMARY UNKNOWN cases
SECTION B) Differential Drills (explicit request only)
SECTION C) Definition cards only (explicit request only)
SECTION D) Mechanism cards
SECTION E) Boards Trap cards
SECTION F) Epidemiology cards (High-Yield pivots only; if present in source)
SECTION G) High-Yield (non-epid, non-definition; includes diagnostic performance + outcomes when present)

Before outputting SECTION A, the assistant MUST already have completed:
â€¢ Core Validation Gate requirements
â€¢ Whole-Source Coverage Audit requirements
â€¢ Core Summary requirements when Pathway A is active

SECTION A CASE LEDGER (MANDATORY BEFORE PREVIEW)

Immediately before the SECTION A Structured Card Preview, the assistant MUST output a PRIMARY UNKNOWN case ledger that accounts for every user-provided intended SECTION A case.

The ledger MUST include:
â€¢ Total user-provided cases: X
â€¢ Eligible for PRIMARY UNKNOWN: Y
â€¢ EXCLUDED from PRIMARY UNKNOWN: Z
â€¢ PRIMARY UNKNOWN cards generated in SECTION A: Y

Then list:
â€¢ every excluded case with its one-line reason

Then explicitly confirm:
â€¢ "SECTION A cardinality check passed: eligible case count matches PRIMARY UNKNOWN count."

Section A case accounting rules:
â€¢ every user-provided intended SECTION A case must be assigned a case index
â€¢ every indexed case must be marked ELIGIBLE or EXCLUDED
â€¢ every EXCLUDED case must carry a one-line allowed reason
â€¢ every ELIGIBLE case must map one-to-one to exactly one PRIMARY UNKNOWN note
â€¢ Section A deduplication is forbidden
â€¢ no eligible case may be merged, substituted, or silently deferred

If the counts do NOT match:
â€¢ STOP
â€¢ Output: "SECTION A blocked - eligible case count does not match PRIMARY UNKNOWN count."
â€¢ Then list the missing or extra case(s) by case index.
â€¢ Do NOT preview SECTION A.

For each section:
â€¢ Output ONLY that sectionâ€™s Structured Card Preview.
â€¢ Do NOT output other sections.
â€¢ Wait for user approval (explicit â€œapprovedâ€ or â€œnext sectionâ€) before proceeding, UNLESS an autonomous generation override is present.
â€¢ If an autonomous generation override is present:
  â†’ section previews are internal validation checkpoints
  â†’ do NOT pause for user approval between sections
  â†’ do NOT output user-facing section-by-section previews unless a hard stop or major repair explanation must be shown

Structured Card Preview rules remain:
â€¢ Do NOT display full summary text anywhere in Review mode.
â€¢ For any RadPrimer/STATdx-origin card: display ONLY the note: â€œSummary field included.â€

-----------------------------------------------------

SECTION-LEVEL COVERAGE ENFORCEMENT (MANDATORY)

Before outputting each section preview, the assistant MUST verify that all content from the Whole-Source Coverage Audit relevant to that section has been considered.

For each section, the assistant must internally check:

â€¢ Which Core-derived items belong in this section
â€¢ Which article-derived items belong in this section
â€¢ Which non-overlapping high-yield items must be included to preserve mastery
â€¢ Which items are intentionally deferred to later sections under the queue rule

No section may be generated from digest-only understanding.
No section may be generated from Core Summary alone.
No section may be generated from summary field text alone.

-----------------------------------------------------

Mode 2 â€” TSV Output (STRICT FORMAT):

â€¢ No header row.
â€¢ Tabs only.
â€¢ One note per line.
â€¢ Column count MUST be exactly:
  22 columns total = 21 fields (core_rad_notetype) + final Tags column.
â€¢ Clinical_Context can NEVER be empty.
â€¢ The final Tags column MUST remain present and MUST be blank / empty for every exported row.
â€¢ If any earlier rule conceptually references tag categories or ontology labels, those references MUST NOT populate the final TSV Tags column.
â€¢ The final TSV Tags column is an intentionally empty placeholder in this workflow.

TSV Structural Integrity Rule (CRITICAL)

â€¢ No literal newline characters (\n or \r) are allowed inside any field.
â€¢ Every note must occupy exactly one physical line in the TSV file.
â€¢ All internal formatting must use <br> instead of newline characters.
â€¢ Any violation invalidates the TSV.

Summary HTML Rule (TSV)
â€¢ The `summary` field must render as simple Anki-safe HTML, not raw markdown.
â€¢ Allowed summary HTML should remain limited to simple tags such as:
  <b> <br> <div> <ul> <ol> <li>
â€¢ The same article-level summary content must be reused identically across all cards from the same article/case bundle.
â€¢ The `summary` field must not contain literal emoji or other non-ASCII icon characters.
â€¢ Any summary icon anchor must be encoded as an HTML numeric character reference, such as `&#x1F50D;`, so TSV text stays ASCII-safe and does not turn into question marks during clipboard/console handling.

-----------------------------------------------------
PREVIEW â†’ TSV IDENTITY LOCK (CRITICAL)

When the user says â€œTSVâ€ (or requests export/download):

1) TSV MUST export EXACTLY the cards shown in the active Structured Card Preview set.
2) The TSV MUST include:
   â€¢ Every HIGH-YIELD (including definition cards)
   â€¢ Every UNKNOWN
   â€¢ Every DIFFERENTIAL DRILL
   â€¢ Every MECHANISM
   â€¢ Every BOARDS TRAP
   â€¢ Any other previewed allowed card types
3) The assistant MUST NOT:
   â€¢ Add new cards
   â€¢ Remove cards
   â€¢ Merge/collapse cards
   â€¢ Substitute â€œexampleâ€ cards
   â€¢ Regenerate a simplified subset
   â€¢ Re-interpret architecture at export time
4) Field content must match the preview content exactly,
   except for minimal formatting adjustments needed to satisfy TSV rules
   (e.g., converting any accidental newlines to <br>).
5) Export order MUST match the preview order exactly
   (canonical order + within-section sequence),
   unless the user explicitly requests a different ordering.

ACTIVE PREVIEW SET DEFINITION (SECTIONED)

â€¢ In standard manual mode:
  â†’ The â€œactive preview setâ€ is the most recent USER-APPROVED section preview.
  â†’ TSV export is allowed ONLY for sections that have been explicitly approved.
  â†’ If a section is modified after approval:
    â†’ that section must be re-previewed and re-approved before TSV export.

â€¢ If an autonomous generation override is present:
  â†’ The â€œactive preview setâ€ is the most recent internally validated section or full-deck snapshot that passed all automatic checks.
  â†’ TSV export is allowed ONLY for sections that have passed automatic validation.
  â†’ If a section is modified after repair:
    â†’ that section must be internally re-validated before TSV export.

COMPLETE PREVIEW REQUIREMENT (MANDATORY)

TSV export is allowed ONLY if the Structured Card Preview explicitly contains:
â€¢ Definition cards ONLY when the user explicitly requests definition cards
â€¢ All intended PRIMARY UNKNOWN cases for the build
â€¢ Any explicitly requested Differential Drills for the build (pattern-distinct, discriminator-distinct, and non-redundant)
â€¢ Any intended Mechanism / Boards Trap / High-Yield (non-ontology)

For SECTION A, "all intended PRIMARY UNKNOWN cases" means:
â€¢ every user-provided intended case has been inventoried
â€¢ every inventoried case is marked ELIGIBLE or EXCLUDED
â€¢ every ELIGIBLE case has exactly one mapped PRIMARY UNKNOWN note
â€¢ every EXCLUDED case has an explicit one-line reason
â€¢ eligible case count exactly matches PRIMARY UNKNOWN count

If the preview is partial, truncated, or missing any intended section:
â€¢ TSV export is prohibited.

If any card cannot be exported due to a structural violation:
â€¢ STOP and output:
  â€œTSV export blocked â€” preview-to-TSV identity lock failed.â€
â€¢ Then list the failing card(s) and the exact violated rule(s).
â€¢ Do NOT output partial TSV.

PRE-TSV CARD COUNT AUDIT (MANDATORY)

Before printing TSV, the assistant MUST state:

â€¢ â€œTotal cards to export: Xâ€
â€¢ â€œExpected TSV lines: Xâ€

Then:
â€¢ The TSV output MUST contain exactly X note lines.

â€¢ STOP and declare mismatch.
â€¢ Do NOT output TSV.

Pre-TSV Validation Gate (MUST PASS BEFORE TSV):

A) Trigger Integrity (Architecture Safety)
Template compatibility guard:
- DIFFERENTIAL DRILL creation must be gated by Differential_Q, not by Differentials.
- Differentials is allowed as UNKNOWN back-side support only after the installed Anki template has been patched to stop generating Differential Drill cards from Differentials alone.
- If the template still gates on Differentials, leave Differentials blank and place the mini-differential in Imaging_Differentiation until the note type is fixed.

â€¢ UNKNOWN cards: Image MUST be populated AND Question MUST be populated.
â€¢ If Image is populated but Question is empty â†’ it must NOT be treated as UNKNOWN.
â€¢ Any non-UNKNOWN card that includes Image MUST keep Question empty.
â€¢ Differential Drill exists ONLY when Differential_Q is populated.
â€¢ Differentials alone may be populated on a PRIMARY UNKNOWN note as back-side differential support.
â€¢ Mechanism exists ONLY when Mechanism is populated.
â€¢ Boards Trap exists ONLY when Boards_Trap is populated.
â€¢ High-Yield exists ONLY when High_Yield_Q is populated.
â€¢ No unintended extra card types may be created by accidental field population.

A1) SECTION A PRIMARY UNKNOWN CARDINALITY (MANDATORY)
â€¢ Every user-provided intended SECTION A case MUST appear in the case inventory.
â€¢ Every inventoried case MUST be marked ELIGIBLE or EXCLUDED.
â€¢ Every EXCLUDED case MUST include a one-line allowed reason.
â€¢ Every ELIGIBLE case MUST map one-to-one to exactly one PRIMARY UNKNOWN note.
â€¢ No two eligible cases may share one PRIMARY UNKNOWN note.
â€¢ No eligible case may be omitted, merged, substituted, or silently deferred.
â€¢ Eligible case count MUST equal PRIMARY UNKNOWN count.

If any of the above fails:
â€¢ STOP
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - SECTION A case ledger / PRIMARY UNKNOWN cardinality rule violated."
â€¢ Then list the missing, extra, or improperly excluded case(s) by case index.
â€¢ Do NOT output TSV.

A2) ONE LEARNING TARGET / ONE TSV ROW INTEGRITY
â€¢ Each note may trigger only one front-driving card family.
â€¢ Separate learning targets MUST be separate TSV rows.
â€¢ UNKNOWN image recognition, entity imaging-pattern recall, report-critical pivots, mechanisms, traps, and explicit differential drills must not be siblings on the same note.
â€¢ If a disease entity needs CT, MRI, ultrasound, nuclear medicine, or report-critical recall, those must be separate High-Yield rows with Question, Differential_Q, Mechanism_Q, Boards_Trap_Q, and non-HY trigger fields blank.

If any row contains multiple independent learning targets or multiple front-driving prompt families:
â€¢ STOP.
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - independent learning targets must be separate TSV rows."
â€¢ Split the note into independent rows and re-run validation.

A3) IMAGE STACK LABEL INTEGRITY
â€¢ Every UNKNOWN Image field MUST include visible non-diagnostic labels in the form "Image 1/N", "Image 2/N", etc.
â€¢ Single-image UNKNOWN cards MUST use "Image 1/1".
â€¢ N MUST equal the number of images in the stack.
â€¢ Labels must not reveal diagnosis, named sign, finding, or anatomy.
â€¢ Labels must not replace captions.
â€¢ Captions must remain off the front.

If an UNKNOWN card lacks correct image-position labels:
â€¢ STOP.
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - image stack label missing or incorrect."
â€¢ Repair the Image field and re-run validation.

A3B) IMAGE MEDIA INTEGRITY WITHOUT VISIBLE BOOKKEEPING
â€¢ Every image-containing note must use exact local media filenames in the Image and Image_Annotated <img src="..."> tags.
â€¢ The validation package, source package, extracted image block, case ledger, media table, metadata, or audit report must preserve the complete image reference name/source identifier for each image.
â€¢ Multi-image notes must preserve one exact image filename/reference per image in source/display order in non-learner-facing audit/media metadata.
â€¢ Do not truncate, paraphrase, normalize, or replace source identifiers in audit/media metadata.
â€¢ Do not put visible image-reference bookkeeping in learner-facing fields.

Forbidden visible learner-facing text includes:
â€¢ "Image reference:"
â€¢ "Image references:"
â€¢ "Source image link(s):"
â€¢ source thumbnail URLs
â€¢ source image URLs
â€¢ local media filename lists used only for bookkeeping

These forbidden blocks must not appear in:
â€¢ Image_Annotated
â€¢ Original_Caption
â€¢ Most_Likely_Diagnosis
â€¢ Differentials
â€¢ Imaging_Differentiation
â€¢ High_Yield_A
â€¢ Mechanism
â€¢ Boards_Trap

If any image-containing note has broken <img src> filenames or lacks audit/media traceability:
â€¢ STOP.
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - image media reference missing or broken."
â€¢ List the failing note(s) and image filename(s).
â€¢ Repair the image src or non-visible audit/media metadata and re-run validation.

If visible image-reference bookkeeping appears in card fields:
â€¢ STOP.
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - visible image bookkeeping leaked into card text."
â€¢ Remove the visible bookkeeping while preserving images and captions.
â€¢ Re-run validation.

A4) ENTITY MODALITY MATRIX COVERAGE
For each disease entity, before TSV export, internally confirm that an entity modality matrix was reviewed:
â€¢ CT noncontrast and CT contrast phases
â€¢ MRI sequences and MRI contrast behavior
â€¢ Ultrasound grayscale, Doppler, dynamic behavior, and CEUS
â€¢ Nuclear medicine / PET / SPECT tracer-specific behavior

If the source explicitly supports a modality appearance pattern:
â€¢ At least one independent entity-level High-Yield appearance row must encode it, unless the pattern is inseparable from another modality/phase card already encoded.
â€¢ Appearance rows are generated per disease entity, not per image.
â€¢ Do not create rows for unsupported modality cells.
â€¢ Do not generalize one contrast agent, phase, sequence, Doppler behavior, CEUS pattern, or radiotracer to another unless source-supported.

If a source-supported entity-level appearance pattern is missing:
â€¢ STOP.
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - source-supported entity imaging pattern not encoded."
â€¢ Generate the missing independent High-Yield row(s) and re-run validation.

A5) REPORT-CRITICAL / MANAGEMENT-PIVOT COVERAGE
If the source names a finding that changes reporting, urgency, immediate communication, intervention, operative planning, staging, resectability, prognosis, risk category, follow-up, complication status, or outcome:
â€¢ At least one independent High-Yield report-critical / management-pivot row must exist, unless the fact is trivial or already fully encoded in a more specific High-Yield row.
â€¢ The question must ask for the specific actionable finding or consequence.
â€¢ Do not export generic "what should you report" cards.

If a source-supported management-changing or report-critical pivot is missing:
â€¢ STOP.
â€¢ Output: "TSV export blocked - report-critical / management-pivot finding not encoded."
â€¢ Generate the missing independent High-Yield row(s) and re-run validation.

A6) HARD QUALITY FILTER
Every exported card must pass at least one of these tests:
â€¢ Helps recognize the image.
â€¢ Helps distinguish the diagnosis from realistic mimics.
â€¢ Teaches source-supported modality-specific appearance.
â€¢ Teaches what must be inspected or reported because it changes management, staging, urgency, prognosis, follow-up, or outcome.

If a card does not pass at least one test:
â€¢ Omit it.
â€¢ Do not replace it with filler.

A7) CLINICAL_CONTEXT ID QUALITY
Every exported row that uses a 12-character Clinical_Context ID must use a random-looking/nonsemantic uppercase alphanumeric ID.
Sequential, monotonic, row-number, or counter-derived IDs are invalid.
Forbidden examples: Q00000000001, Q00000000002, CASE000000001, ROW000000001, UNKNOWN000001.
If any row uses a counter-style ID:
- STOP.
- Output: "TSV export blocked - Clinical_Context ID is sequential, not random-looking."
- Replace the ID with a random-looking 12-character uppercase alphanumeric ID and re-run validation.

B) Source Coverage + Non-Fabrication
â€¢ All board-relevant Core content for the topic is represented (without trivial duplication), unless Core GAP pathway is invoked.
â€¢ All board-relevant RadPrimer/STATdx content for the topic is represented (when provided).
â€¢ All high-value non-overlapping article content needed for durable mastery is represented when article-based generation is being used.
â€¢ Whole-Source Coverage Audit requirements were completed before section generation.
â€¢ If Pathway A was used, Core Summary requirements were completed before SECTION A.
â€¢ If Pathway A was used, the final response contains the exact CORE_EVIDENCE_FILE_BEGIN / CORE_EVIDENCE_FILE_END block with Core source basis, Core facts used, derived card coverage, and limitations.
â€¢ No fabricated:
  imaging findings
  MRI signal/sequence behavior
  ultrasound behavior (echogenicity, echotexture, posterior acoustic behavior, Doppler behavior, compressibility, mobility, wall layering, maneuver findings, or source-named sonographic signs/artifacts)
  differentials (EXCEPT explicitly labeled Model-inferred differentials permitted under CONTROLLED INFERENCE)
  epidemiology
  thresholds / staging / management triggers
â€¢ If anything is inferred (rare): it MUST be explicitly labeled:
  â€œNot explicitly listed in Core/RadPrimer/STATdx; inferred based on imaging pattern.â€

C) Image/Captions â†’ Keyword Findings Integrity (UNKNOWN ONLY)
â€¢ For UNKNOWN cards:
  Caption-derived KEYWORD FINDINGS should be present in the visible UNKNOWN back, preferably in Imaging_Differentiation under "Key imaging findings:" so the template can render diagnosis and differential first.
  Most_Likely_Diagnosis may contain keyword findings only when the template must parse legacy card content.
  Those phrases must be explicitly present in the verbatim captions
  No inference beyond caption text
â€¢ Image_Annotated order must match Image order (when present).

D) Differential Drill De-duplication (Pattern-Based; Discriminator-Driven; Explicit Request Only)

Core Principle:
Standalone drill generation is disabled unless the user explicitly requests separate differential drill cards.
When explicitly requested, drill generation is determined by Dominant Imaging Pattern and Primary Discriminator Logic â€” NOT by exact differential list equality.

STEP 1 â€” Identify Dominant Pattern
â€¢ For each potential Differential Drill, explicitly classify the dominant imaging pattern.
â€¢ The dominant pattern is defined by the primary discriminator that separates the leading entities.
â€¢ Examples:
  - Calcified Encasing Peritoneum Pattern
  - Smooth Diffuse Enhancing Peritoneum Pattern
  - Gas-Containing Ascites Pattern
  - Solid Polypoid Mass Pattern
  - Circumferential Stricture Pattern
  - Post-Operative Leak Pattern

STEP 2 â€” Deduplication Rule
â€¢ If two cases share the SAME dominant pattern AND the SAME primary discriminator logic:
  â†’ Generate ONLY ONE Differential Drill.
  â†’ All other cases must leave Differential_Q blank.
  â†’ Differential lists and discriminator reasoning may still appear on the associated UNKNOWN back in Differentials and Imaging_Differentiation.

â€¢ If two cases differ in dominant pattern OR require a different primary discriminator strategy:
  â†’ A new Differential Drill is ALLOWED.
  â†’ Overlap in differential lists does NOT prevent generation.

STEP 3 â€” Inflation Suppression
Do NOT generate a new drill if:
â€¢ The same mental separation strategy would be repeated.
â€¢ The same dominant discriminator is being trained.
â€¢ Only minor list variation exists without a new cognitive pivot.

STEP 4 â€” Justification Requirement
If generating a new drill in a topic where another drill already exists,
Imaging_Differentiation MUST begin with:
â€œDominant pattern differs from other cases because: [distinct discriminator explanation].â€

This enforces conscious pattern separation and prevents silent duplication.

Key Rule:
Pattern distinctiveness overrides list variation.
Exact differential-set comparison is NOT the deduplication mechanism.

E) Differential Drill Structural Enforcement (CRITICAL)
Applies to every note where Differential_Q is populated:

Required Field Presence + Isolation
â€¢ Differential_Q MUST be populated.
â€¢ Imaging_Differentiation MUST be populated.
â€¢ Question MUST be blank.
â€¢ Mechanism_Q / Mechanism MUST be blank.
â€¢ Boards_Trap_Q / Boards_Trap MUST be blank.
â€¢ High_Yield_Q / High_Yield_A MUST be blank.

Mode Determination (MANDATORY)
â€¢ If a drill originates from an existing UNKNOWN case:
  â†’ This Differential Drill MUST be MODE 1 (case-bundled).
  â†’ Standalone mode is NOT allowed.
â€¢ If no such UNKNOWN exists:
  â†’ MODE 2 may be used (rare), but must satisfy Mode 2 rules below.

MODE 1 (Case-Bundled) Requirements
â€¢ Clinical_Context MUST preserve the same blinded clinical wording as the associated UNKNOWN card, but MUST be unique at the note level to prevent duplicate-note conflicts.
â€¢ A short deterministic suffix (e.g., [DD1]) is allowed and expected for case-bundled Differential Drills.
â€¢ Image MUST be populated and MUST match the associated UNKNOWN Image exactly.
â€¢ If the associated UNKNOWN card contains Image_Annotated (non-empty):
  â†’ The Differential Drill MUST include the exact same Image_Annotated block
    (same stackItem blocks + verbatim captions).
  â†’ Missing Image_Annotated in this scenario = structural failure.
â€¢ Entity_Label is RECOMMENDED and must contain diagnosis only (no descriptors/findings/reasoning).
â€¢ Most_Likely_Diagnosis must be blank OR diagnosis-only (single line). No keyword findings or reasoning.

MODE 2 (Standalone) Requirements (RARE)
â€¢ Clinical_Context MUST be a unique 12-character alphanumeric/symbolic ID and NOTHING else.
â€¢ Entity_Label is REQUIRED (diagnosis-only).
â€¢ Image is OPTIONAL.
â€¢ Most_Likely_Diagnosis must be blank OR diagnosis-only (single line).

Front-Side Blinding Audit (Differential Drill)
â€¢ Front may contain ONLY:
  Clinical_Context
  Differential_Q
  Image stack (if present)
â€¢ Front must NOT contain:
  diagnosis names (except neutral Entity_Label if template prints it)
  named differentials
  caption-derived keyword findings
  named signs
  management clues

If ANY requirement in Section E fails:
â€¢ Do NOT output TSV.
â€¢ Fix the violating note(s) and re-run the validation gate.
â€¢ Any card modifications during this fix step REQUIRE a full re-render of Structured Card Preview (canonical order) before TSV export.

F) TSV EXPORT BLOCKER â€” CAPTION INTEGRITY
If any Image_Annotated caption differs from the provided caption by even 1 character:
â€¢ STOP
â€¢ Output: â€œTSV export blocked â€” caption verbatim rule violated.â€
â€¢ List the offending filename(s).
â€¢ Do NOT output TSV.

G) SECTION F â€” EPIDEMIOLOGY EMISSION RULE (HIGH-YIELD ONLY)
During SECTION F:
â€¢ Output ONLY Epidemiology High-Yield cards that change image interpretation, differential ranking, reporting, or management.
â€¢ Every SECTION F card MUST use:
  - High_Yield_Q
  - High_Yield_A
â€¢ All other fields MUST be blank.

including the required â€œExam pivot: â€¦â€ line.

Generic epidemiology or "most common" trivia is not enough.

No diagnostic performance numbers in SECTION F.
No outcomes/time-to-intervention cards in SECTION F.
Those belong ONLY in SECTION G.

N) High-Yield Hygiene
- Structural subtype/framework pivots are allowed ONLY when source-supported, compact, and interpretation-changing.
â€¢ High-Yield cards must be true decision pivots only:
  modality-specific appearance, contrast/tracer behavior, differential discriminators, pitfalls, thresholds, staging rules, management triggers, classification pivots, structural subtype/framework pivots, diagnostic performance pivots, outcome/time pivots, or interpretation-changing epidemiology pivots.
â€¢ No trivial epidemiology; include epidemiology only if it changes interpretation and is source-supported.
â€¢ Do not export generic "most common" or prevalence cards unless the answer explicitly states the radiology pivot.
â€¢ If a High-Yield card is true but would not make the user better at reading studies, delete it or rewrite it into a modality pattern, discriminator, pitfall, or report-critical card.

O) FINAL DECK-BALANCE AUDIT (MANDATORY)
Before TSV export, explicitly check the final approved deck for balance:
â€¢ Are there too many definition cards relative to true recognition/discriminator cards?
â€¢ If definition cards were explicitly requested, are they limited to definitions that change diagnosis, differential ranking, reporting, risk, or management?
â€¢ Are there enough image-recognition cards for the provided cases?

â€¢ Are modality pivots represented when source-supported (MRI / ultrasound / nuclear medicine / CT as applicable)?
â€¢ Are management and risk cards present but not crowding out recognition?
â€¢ Are compare/contrast cards limited to decision-changing comparisons?

If the deck is visibly imbalanced toward low-yield definitions, repetitive management pivots, or duplicated recognition moves:

P0B) SOURCE-BOOKKEEPING CARD BAN (MANDATORY)

- "what was included"

- "what does the prompt require"
- "what information was extracted"
- "was diagnostic performance provided"

ULTRASOUND-SPECIFIC COVERAGE + EXPORT VALIDATION

Q) ULTRASOUND NON-FABRICATION RULE (STRICT)
If ultrasound appearance is discussed anywhere in the build:
- Do NOT invent echogenicity.
- Do NOT invent echotexture.
- Do NOT invent posterior acoustic enhancement or shadowing.
- Do NOT invent Doppler flow or vascularity.
- Do NOT invent compressibility.
- Do NOT invent wall layering or stratification.
- Do NOT invent mobility or maneuver findings.
- Do NOT invent contrast-enhanced ultrasound enhancement or washout behavior.
- Do NOT invent twinkle, ring-down, comet-tail, reverberation, sludge/debris behavior, or any other ultrasound-specific artifact/sign.

All ultrasound content must remain explicitly source-supported.

-----------------------------------------------------

R) ULTRASOUND APPEARANCE COVERAGE
If the source text explicitly describes ultrasound appearance for the entity/topic:
- At least ONE High-Yield ultrasound appearance card MUST exist in the approved preview set.

If no such card exists:
- STOP.
- Output: "TSV export blocked - ultrasound appearance described in source but not encoded."
- Then generate the missing ultrasound appearance High-Yield card(s) in SECTION G and re-preview.

If an autonomous generation override is present:
- "approved preview set" means the most recent automatically validated section or full-deck snapshot.
- "re-preview" means internally re-validate after repair, not pause for user approval.

-----------------------------------------------------

S) ULTRASOUND APPEARANCE REVIEW SCOPE
Ultrasound appearance may include, when explicitly source-supported:
- echogenicity
- echotexture
- posterior acoustic enhancement or shadowing
- Doppler vascularity
- compressibility
- cystic-versus-solid morphology
- source-stated sonographic signs or artifacts
- ultrasound-specific discriminator features
- ultrasound-specific exam pivots
- contrast-enhanced ultrasound enhancement phase or washout behavior

These details must not be generalized beyond the source.

NUCLEAR MEDICINE-SPECIFIC COVERAGE + EXPORT VALIDATION

T) NUCLEAR MEDICINE NON-FABRICATION RULE (STRICT)
If nuclear medicine appearance is discussed anywhere in the build:
- Do NOT invent tracer uptake.
- Do NOT invent photopenia or warm uptake.
- Do NOT invent PET avidity.
- Do NOT invent retention, washout, excretion, timing, or phase behavior.
- Do NOT invent source-specific scintigraphic patterns.
- Do NOT infer one tracer's behavior from another tracer's behavior.

All nuclear medicine content must remain explicitly source-supported.

-----------------------------------------------------

U) NUCLEAR MEDICINE APPEARANCE COVERAGE
If the source text explicitly describes nuclear medicine appearance for the entity/topic:
- At least ONE High-Yield nuclear medicine appearance card MUST exist in the approved preview set.

If no such card exists:
- STOP.
- Output: "TSV export blocked - nuclear medicine appearance described in source but not encoded."
- Then generate the missing nuclear medicine appearance High-Yield card(s) in SECTION G and re-preview.

If an autonomous generation override is present:
- "approved preview set" means the most recent automatically validated section or full-deck snapshot.
- "re-preview" means internally re-validate after repair, not pause for user approval.

-----------------------------------------------------

V) NUCLEAR MEDICINE APPEARANCE REVIEW SCOPE
Nuclear medicine appearance may include, when explicitly source-supported:
- tracer uptake or non-uptake
- photopenic versus warm behavior
- PET avidity
- source-stated scintigraphic distribution
- source-stated retention, washout, excretion, timing, or phase behavior
- physiologic biodistribution that creates a pitfall
- tracer-specific discriminator features
- nuclear medicine-specific exam pivots

These details must not be generalized beyond the source.

=== ARTICLE ===
TITLE: Focal Liver Lesion With Hemorrhage

DIFFERENTIAL DIAGNOSIS [RadPrimer]
- Common
  - Hepatic Trauma
  - Hepatic Adenoma
  - Hepatocellular Carcinoma
  - Hepatic Cyst
  - Autosomal Dominant Polycystic Disease, Liver
- Less Common
  - Coagulopathic Hemorrhage, Liver
  - Hepatic Metastases
  - HELLP Syndrome
  - Amyloidosis

ESSENTIAL INFORMATION [Both]
- Key Differential Diagnosis Issues
  - Hemorrhage may be detected as heterogeneous high attenuation (> 60 HU on NECT), or high-intensity foci on T1WI and T2WI
  - Bleeding may include subcapsular and intraperitoneal extension
- Helpful Clues for Common Diagnoses
  - Hepatic Trauma
    - Blunt or penetrating (including biopsies, TIPS, etc.)
    - Hepatic lacerations usually have linear or stellate configuration
    - Location: Right lobe (75%), left lobe (25%)
    - Best imaging tool: Dual-phase CT in hemodynamically stable patients
  - Hepatic Adenoma
    - Foci of hemorrhage within tumor is common feature on MR, less common on CT
    - Spontaneous bleeding within or around hepatic mass in young female without cirrhosis is almost diagnostic of adenoma
    - Other signs of adenoma
      - Lipid or fat content
      - Multiplicity
      - Encapsulation
  - Hepatocellular Carcinoma
    - Spontaneous hemorrhage within tumor is uncommon
    - Spontaneous rupture through capsule is relatively common for large hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC)
    - Other signs of HCC
      - Occurrence within cirrhotic liver
      - Hypervascularity with washout
      - Encapsulation
  - Hepatic Cyst
    - Isolated or part of autosomal dominant polycystic disease
    - Clotted blood in cyst may be mistaken for tumor but will not show enhancement
- Helpful Clues for Less Common Diagnoses
  - Coagulopathic Hemorrhage, Liver
    - Spontaneous intrahepatic or perihepatic hemorrhage is rare manifestation of coagulopathy or anticoagulant therapy
    - Spherical hematoma within liver may simulate tumor
    - Others signs of coagulopathic hemorrhage
      - Hematocrit sign (fluid level) within hematoma
      - Multiple sites of bleeding
      - Favored sites: Iliopsoas and rectus muscles
  - Hepatic Metastases
    - Hemorrhage is uncommon, usually associated with hypervascular metastases
    - May occur following chemotherapy or transhepatic ablation of metastatic lesions
    - Most commonly: Lung cancer, renal cell carcinoma, pancreatic neuroendocrine tumor, and melanoma
  - HELLP Syndrome
    - Hemolysis, elevated liver enzymes, low platelets
    - Severe variation of toxemia of pregnancy
    - Intrahepatic or subcapsular fluid collection (hematoma)
      - Occasionally active extravasation
    - Wedge-shaped areas of infarction
  - Amyloidosis
    - Hepatocellular rupture extremely rare

=== SOURCE-GROUNDED IMAGE AND CAPTION DEPTH ===
[Both] Hepatic trauma: STATdx image 1 demonstrates laceration, active bleeding, hemoperitoneum and rib fractures. STATdx image 2 shows a dense biopsy tract and subcapsular hematoma after a fall in hematocrit. [STATdx] STATdx image 3 supplies a very similar biopsy-related view, retained because literal identity remains uncertain.
[Both] Adenoma-pattern images span subcapsular bleeding with a hypervascular mass (STATdx image 4), central hyperdense intratumoral blood on NECT (STATdx image 5) and bright foci on T1 MRI (STATdx image 6). [STATdx] STATdx image 7 adds a visually distinct fat-suppressed T1 example and explicitly names hepatic adenoma.
[Both] HCC: STATdx image 8 and STATdx image 9 are explicitly the same patient, showing different arterial-phase levels with tumor vessels, ascites, sentinel clot and capsular rupture. The second caption reports angiographic confirmation and coil embolization; no angiographic frame from that HCC case is supplied. STATdx image 10 adds NECT and STATdx image 11 a coronal example in other described patients.
[Both] Hemorrhagic cysts: STATdx image 12 shows denser material in a thin-walled cyst; STATdx image 13 shows organizing clot and fibrin strands by ultrasound; STATdx image 14 shows dependent T2-dark material described as subacute hemorrhage. Do not assume these are the same patient. STATdx image 15 contrasts T1-dark simple-fluid cysts with T1-bright hemorrhagic cysts in polycystic liver.
[Both] Coagulopathy: STATdx image 16 demonstrates a hematocrit level, active bleeding and multisite hepatic/renal hemorrhage. STATdx image 17 shows anticoagulant-associated fracture-like defects without trauma; resolution after medication withdrawal and absence of an underlying mass are source-reported follow-up, with no follow-up frame supplied.
[Both] Metastases: STATdx image 18 shows spontaneous subcapsular bleeding from a pancreatic neuroendocrine metastasis. The melanoma caption for STATdx image 19 says the high density perhaps indicates bleeding; preserve that uncertainty. STATdx image 20 provides sentinel clot and extensive hemoperitoneum, reported as 35 HU. Preserve these visually related melanoma views together without declaring a confirmed same-patient series.
[Both] HELLP: STATdx image 21 shows massive subcapsular/perihepatic hemorrhage and active bleeding; STATdx image 22 shows postpartum hematoma with infarcts. [STATdx] STATdx image 23 adds selective arteriography, medial liver displacement by hematoma, active hemorrhage and a caption-reported coil-embolization treatment. The precise CT-to-angiogram patient link is not supplied; retain the whole comparison/procedure set.
[RadPrimer] Amyloidosis remains in the less-common differential. [Both] The text describes rupture as extremely rare; neither staged image set contains a labeled amyloidosis example.

=== INTERPRETATION AND SOURCE LIMITS ===
[Both] The source uses > 60 HU on NECT as a possible appearance of hemorrhage. The 35-HU hemoperitoneum in the melanoma caption is a different case/context; preserve both without inventing a universal attenuation threshold.
[Both] The source describes T1/T2 hyperintense hemorrhagic foci, while the cyst caption also describes T2-hypointense dependent blood. Keep the stated sequence and case/stage context. The corresponding T2 images mentioned in the T1 adenoma captions are not staged; do not claim to have inspected them.
[Both] The adenoma wording almost diagnostic and the caption attribution of T1/T2 signal to hemorrhage rather than fat are preserved source statements. They must not be generalized beyond their supplied clinical/sequence context or presented as independently validated rules.
[Both] Embolization and medication withdrawal are reported outcomes in specific source cases. No treatment algorithm, dose, risk threshold or independent management guideline is supplied.

=== ATOMIC IMAGE GROUPS ===
STATdx image 2, STATdx image 3: Very similar anatomy and biopsy-related hemorrhage; exact slice identity is uncertain. Keep both views. Biopsy and falling hematocrit are source history, not an imaged time-lapse.
STATdx image 6, STATdx image 7: Distinct T1 and fat-suppressed T1 examples; patient identity and acquisition relationship are not established. Preserve both for comparison without asserting a same-patient sequence.
STATdx image 8, STATdx image 9: Caption explicitly states same patient. Two different arterial-phase slices show ruptured HCC and sentinel clot. Angiographic confirmation and coil embolization are reported, but no HCC angiogram or post-treatment frame is staged.
STATdx image 19, STATdx image 20: Closely matching lesion/organ anatomy suggests companion levels, but captions do not explicitly confirm same patient. Keep together without assigning a verified patient identity. Possible intratumoral bleeding in the first view remains qualified; the second shows stronger sentinel-clot/hemoperitoneum evidence.
STATdx image 21, STATdx image 22, STATdx image 23: Keep both obstetric CT examples and the selective hepatic arteriogram together. The angiogram caption says this woman, but its exact CT patient link is unspecified; do not assert that all three images show one patient. Active hemorrhage and coil embolization are source-reported; no post-embolization endpoint image is supplied.
No source case cluster was intentionally split. No imaged time-lapse or complete before/after treatment sequence is supplied.
